# TL;DR

Electron app built using Bun, just like how Claude Code and Codex.

## Language: TypeScript

## Storage: GitHub
(initially using 'data' folder in this repo)

The system of record inside the data repo is a set of Automerge (CRDT)
documents, one per feature, stored as content-addressed binary chunks under
`.specdriven/automerge/` and synced through the same git pull/push as
everything else. The `.feature.md` files are deterministic projections of
those documents — still the human-readable, PR-reviewable artifact on
GitHub. Concurrent edits merge at the CRDT level (character-level for
scenario bodies) instead of surfacing as git conflicts; markdown edited
outside the app (an older app version, a GitHub PR) is reconciled back into
the documents on the next sync tick. Optionally, Settings can name a
websocket sync server (e.g. wss://sync.automerge.org) that delivers the
same operations in near-real time — off by default, git stays the source of
record. See specs/automerge-store.spec.md.

## Authorized users:
Users that have read/write access to the repo that stores data. 

## Local state

`.specdriven/settings.json`, in the project root or in `$HOME`
(specs/app-start.spec.md)

# Integration with AI coding agents

"Start session" builds one prompt from the selected specs and hands it to an
agent — Claude Code Desktop, or the app's own pane driven by Claude, by Pi or
by Codex (src/server/agent.ts). Desktop: the full prompt and a `session.json` record are
written under
`<settings dir>/sessions/<uuid>/` — beside `.specdriven/settings.json`, never
in the auto-pushed data repo — and the app opens
`claude://code/new?q=<bootstrap>&folder=<data repo>`, a short prompt that tells
the new Claude Code Desktop session to title itself with the session name and
work from the prompt file. The deep link and its limits (`q` capped at 14336
chars) were read from the shipped Claude.app bundle, not from public docs, so
they are version-coupled.

Pane: for developers with the Claude CLI but not Claude Code Desktop, the app
drives a live Claude Code session itself (src/server/agentChat.ts,
src/frontend/AgentPane.tsx). One long-lived `query()` from
`@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk` per chat, in streaming-input mode, spawns the
developer's own `claude` binary with the project's `code-repo` (else the data
repo) as its working directory and Claude Code's own system prompt and
settings. The SDK's messages become an in-memory event log with sequence
numbers; the browser reads it over a one-way NDJSON stream from the same route
table (no WebSocket, so it works over Electron's `app://` too) and reopens the
stream from the last sequence it saw. Permission requests and clarifying
questions round-trip through cards in the pane; the "/" popup lists whatever
`supportedCommands()` reports for the session. A `session.json` with
`agent: "pane"` and Claude's own session id (`agentSessionId`) goes under
`<settings dir>/sessions/<uuid>/`, so resuming a past pane session is a cheap
follow-up. When a project names a
`code-repo`, the session runs there and the data repo is an additional
directory, so Claude can read the specs the prompt points at.

Pi: the same pane, driven by pi.dev's agent instead (src/server/piChat.ts,
specs/agents/integration-with-pi-dev-agent.feature.md), offered when the `pi`
binary is on PATH. `pi --mode rpc` speaks JSON lines on stdin/stdout: commands
out (`prompt`, `abort`, `get_state`, `get_commands`), a `response` per command
correlated by id, and a stream of events in. Those events are translated into
the very same event log Claude's messages fill, so the transcript, the browser
stream and the session record are shared; the record says `agent: "pi-pane"`.
Framing is strict JSONL — split on `\n` only, strip a trailing `\r`, never a
generic line reader, since U+2028 is legal inside JSON strings. Pi's RPC mode
has no permission protocol: it runs its own tools, so those sessions raise no
permission cards, offer no permission mode, and say so in the transcript.
Extension dialogs, which would otherwise block pi forever, are cancelled with
a note.

Codex: the same pane, driven by OpenAI's Codex CLI (src/server/codexChat.ts),
offered when the `codex` binary is on PATH. `codex app-server` speaks
JSON-RPC-shaped lines on stdin/stdout: requests out (`initialize`,
`thread/start`, `turn/start`, `turn/interrupt`, `turn/steer`), a response per
request correlated by id, notifications in (`item/started`,
`item/agentMessage/delta`, `item/completed`, `turn/completed`, `error`) and —
unlike pi — requests *from* the server that block the turn until answered:
`item/commandExecution/requestApproval`, `item/fileChange/requestApproval`,
`item/tool/requestUserInput`. The notifications are translated into the shared
event log (a command or file change is a tool row, an agent message a streamed
reply, `turn/completed` the turn footer); the server requests become the same
permission and question cards Claude raises, answered with Codex's decisions
(`accept`, `acceptForSession` for "Allow always", `decline`, `cancel` on a
Stop). Codex has no permission modes but an approval policy and a sandbox, so
the pane's modes map onto those (default → on-request + workspace-write,
acceptEdits → on-failure, plan → read-only, bypass → never + full access) and
travel with every `turn/start`; a change applies from the next turn. The
protocol shapes were read out of `codex app-server generate-ts` for Codex CLI
0.141, so they are version-coupled. The record says `agent: "codex-pane"` and
carries Codex's thread id as `agentSessionId`; the thread is named after the
session, so `codex resume` lists it by that name.

The feature spec lives in the spec repo as
`specs/agents/coding-agent-ui.feature.md`; the spec repo is never edited from
this one.

## Why not RDBMS for data ?

We want to make this application very easy to adopt.
Provisioning an RDBMS server maybe a source of friction in some
environments.

# Automerge system of record

The data repo's system of record is a set of Automerge (CRDT) documents, one
per feature. The `.feature.md` files that render on GitHub are deterministic
projections of those documents — same doc, same bytes, on every peer.

Each scenario in a document carries a `status` field: `draft`, `proposed`, or
`published` (crud-feature.feature.md "Scenario status"). Only proposed and
published scenarios are projected into the `.feature.md`; a draft lives in
the document alone.

## Storage layout

Each document lives inside the data repo under `.specdriven/automerge/`, in
a tree that mirrors the repo's own top-level folders: a document's directory
is its source file's path, with `models/` reading as `event-models/`, plus
one fixed directory for the test management module's document, which has no
markdown side.

```
specs/policy/auto-policy.feature.md                    <- projection
.specdriven/automerge/
    specs/policy/auto-policy.feature.md/               <- the feature's document
        docid                                          <- shared identity
        snapshot-<hash>.bin                            <- full document
        changes-<hash>.bin                             <- incremental changes
    roadmap/<name>.md/                                 <- roadmap pages
    tasks/<name>.md/                                   <- tasks
    notebook/<name>.md/                                <- notebook entries
    event-models/<path>.model.json/                    <- Event Models (models/)
    test-management/project/                           <- test management
```

Chunk files are content-addressed and write-once, so two peers pushing
concurrently never collide on a path: a git merge of their pushes is a plain
union of files, and loading merges every chunk into one document. Once
enough change files pile up, a save folds them into a fresh snapshot and
deletes them from the working tree (compaction).

## Decisions

Git stays the required transport and GitHub access stays the whole
authorization model (ARCHITECTURE.md): the documents ride the same clone,
pull and push as the markdown. No sync server is required; the optional
websocket sync below accelerates delivery without changing the source of
record.

## A feature file deleted outside the app @v1 [published]

When a git pull removes a feature file, the next sync tick removes its document directory too — the markdown is the interchange format, and its deletion carries the intent.

## a repo that predates documents @v1 [proposed]

Feature files with no document behind them behave as before until touched:
the first save, publish, or sync tick imports them (the document is minted
from the parsed markdown). Nothing breaks for collaborators on older app
versions — their plain-markdown pushes keep working and are absorbed as
drift. If two peers import the same feature concurrently, the merge picks
one document identity deterministically and folds both sides' content in.

## Documents from before the mirrored layout @v1 [proposed]

Earlier app versions filed documents under a directory per kind —
`features/`, `docs/`, `models/`, `testing/` — with the source file's whole
path beneath it, so every folder name appeared twice
(`features/specs/...`, `models/models/...`). On the first sync tick after
the upgrade, every document found under those roots is moved to its place
in the mirrored layout, identity file included, and the moves land in one
commit of renames, so nothing about a document changes but where it sits.
A document that already has a directory at the new place — a teammate
migrated first, or pushed from an older version since — is merged into it
chunk by chunk: chunk names are content hashes, so a name already there is
the same bytes, and loading still merges every chunk into one document. The
old roots disappear once nothing is left in them.

## Documents from before the status field @v1 [published]

Documents written by earlier app versions carried `published`/`proposed`
booleans per scenario instead of `status`. Loading such a document migrates
it in place — booleans fold into the status field, unmarked scenarios become
drafts — and the migration persists with the document's next save.

## draft scenarios live in the document only @v1 [proposed]

A draft scenario is internal: it is stored in the feature's document but
never written to the `.feature.md`, so the published spec on GitHub shows
only proposed and published scenarios. The editor exchanges drafts with the
server as an explicit `[draft]` chip on the heading — a wire format the
projection never writes to a file — and reading a feature folds the
document's drafts back into what the editor shows. A chipless heading
hand-added to the markdown is absorbed as a draft — into the document, out
of the file — on the next sync tick. Because markdown cannot see drafts, markdown-driven
reconciliation never deletes them: a draft leaves the document only when
something that can see it (the document itself) removes or promotes it. A
`[proposed]` chip on the draft's name and version promotes the existing
entry in place, keeping its identity and edit history. Deleting a draft is
the other way out: the app removes the entry from the document by name and
version — the deletion markdown cannot express — and the reload cron commits
and pushes the document as it does any other saved change. The operation is
for drafts only; a proposed scenario is deleted by saving the feature
without it. Concurrent status
changes merge as a one-way latch: the side further along
draft → proposed → published wins.

## markdown edited outside the app @v1 [proposed]

A feature file edited where no document ops exist — an older app version, a
hand edit merged through a GitHub PR — drifts from its document's
projection. The next sync tick absorbs the drift into the document (a
scenario-level three-way merge against the git merge base when the pull
conflicted) and rewrites the file to the canonical projection. The GitHub
PR workflow stays a first-class way to edit specs.

## optional live sync over websocket @v1 [proposed]

Settings can name a sync server (e.g. wss://sync.automerge.org). While one
is named, every feature document is announced to it under an identity all
peers derive from the docid file, local saves stream out as CRDT operations
in near-real time, and arriving operations merge into the chunk store and
rewrite the markdown projection — the next sync tick commits them, so git
remains the source of record. Double delivery over both transports is
harmless: merging the same operations twice is a no-op. The setting is off
by default and the Settings page warns that on a public server anyone
holding a document's id can read that document; peers with private data
point it at a self-hosted sync server instead.

## published scenarios stay immutable @v1 [proposed]

Drift that changes the body of a published scenario is refused: the file is
repaired from the document and the repair is committed, so tampering with
published history — accidental or otherwise — does not stick. In-app writes
are refused at save time, as before. An explicit human conflict resolution
is the one door that may override the document's published state.

## two peers edit the same scenario concurrently @v1 [proposed]

Peer A and peer B both edit the body of the same scenario, even the same
line. Each save becomes CRDT operations in the feature's document. When
their pushes meet in a git pull, the markdown conflicts — but the sync tick
resolves it from the merged document, character-level, with no conflict
dialog. Both edits survive on both peers, byte-identical.

=== merge.ts
// Scenario-level three-way merge of parsed features. This is the bridge for
// edits that arrive as plain markdown with no CRDT ops behind them — an older
// app version's push, or a hand edit on GitHub — merged against the common
// ancestor the way git would, but at the granularity of the domain model:
// per scenario, not per line. Peers running this app version don't usually
// need it (their ops ride the chunk files and merge in the document itself);
// it is the fallback that keeps mixed-version teams and the PR workflow safe.

import * as A from "@automerge/automerge";
import type {
  EntryKind,
  ExternalLink,
  Feature,
  MarkdownPage,
  Scenario,
  Supersedes,
} from "../parser";

export type ThreeWayResult =
  | { ok: true; feature: Feature }
  | { ok: false; conflict: string };

const key = (s: Scenario) => `${s.name}@v${s.version}`;

function byKey(f: Feature): Map<string, Scenario> {
  return new Map(f.scenarios.map((s) => [key(s), s]));
}

/**
 * Character-level three-way text merge, with Automerge as the diff3 engine:
 * both sides' rewrites replay as updateText edits on forks of the base and
 * merge as CRDT operations. Overlapping edits both apply — the same outcome
 * concurrent in-app edits get — instead of surfacing a line conflict.
 */
function mergeText(base: string, ours: string, theirs: string): string {
  if (theirs === base || theirs === ours) return ours;
  if (ours === base) return theirs;
  const root = A.from<{ t: string }>({ t: base });
  const oursDoc = A.change(A.clone(root), (d) => A.updateText(d, ["t"], ours));
  const theirsDoc = A.change(A.clone(root), (d) => A.updateText(d, ["t"], theirs));
  return A.merge(oursDoc, theirsDoc).t;
}

/**
 * The same three-way merge for a markdown page — a roadmap page, a task, a
 * notebook entry (specs/app/engineering-notebook.feature.md). A page has no
 * scenarios, so there is nothing to match up by key: the heading and the body
 * merge as text, and each metadata field is one value, where the side that
 * changed it wins and ours wins when both did — the rule a feature's
 * assignment already follows. A page merge therefore never conflicts.
 */
export function threeWayPage(
  base: MarkdownPage,
  ours: MarkdownPage,
  theirs: MarkdownPage,
): MarkdownPage {
  const pick = <T>(b: T | undefined, o: T | undefined, t: T | undefined, same: (x?: T, y?: T) => boolean): T | undefined =>
    !same(t, b) && same(o, b) ? t : o;
  const eq = (x?: string, y?: string) => (x ?? "") === (y ?? "");
  const eqBool = (x?: boolean, y?: boolean) => (x ?? false) === (y ?? false);
  const eqNum = (x?: number, y?: number) => (x ?? 0) === (y ?? 0);
  const eqLink = (x?: Supersedes, y?: Supersedes) =>
    (x?.title ?? "") === (y?.title ?? "") && (x?.url ?? "") === (y?.url ?? "");
  const merged: MarkdownPage = {
    name: mergeText(base.name, ours.name, theirs.name),
    body: mergeText(base.body, ours.body, theirs.body),
  };
  const assigned = pick(base.assigned, ours.assigned, theirs.assigned, eq);
  const estimate = pick(base.estimate, ours.estimate, theirs.estimate, eqNum);
  const done = pick(base.done, ours.done, theirs.done, eqBool);
  const author = pick(base.author, ours.author, theirs.author, eq);
  const date = pick(base.date, ours.date, theirs.date, eq);
  const kind = pick(base.kind, ours.kind, theirs.kind, eq as (x?: EntryKind, y?: EntryKind) => boolean);
  const supersedes = pick(base.supersedes, ours.supersedes, theirs.supersedes, eqLink);
  if (assigned) merged.assigned = assigned;
  if (estimate) merged.estimate = estimate;
  if (done) merged.done = true;
  if (author) merged.author = author;
  if (date) merged.date = date;
  if (kind) merged.kind = kind;
  if (supersedes) merged.supersedes = supersedes;
  return merged;
}

/**
 * threeWayPage for a save whose base is only a guess (pages.ts absorb, when
 * the caller sends none): as threeWayFeatureStrict, a text both sides changed
 * to different values is a conflict rather than a character merge, since the
 * caller may already hold the other side's edit and would get it twice.
 */
export function threeWayPageStrict(
  base: MarkdownPage,
  ours: MarkdownPage,
  theirs: MarkdownPage,
): { ok: true; page: MarkdownPage } | { ok: false; conflict: string } {
  const clash = (b: string, o: string, t: string) => o !== b && t !== b && o !== t;
  if (clash(base.name, ours.name, theirs.name)) {
    return { ok: false, conflict: "the heading was changed on both sides" };
  }
  if (clash(base.body, ours.body, theirs.body)) {
    return { ok: false, conflict: "the text was changed on both sides" };
  }
  return { ok: true, page: threeWayPage(base, ours, theirs) };
}

/** draft → proposed → published: the lifecycle only moves forward. */
const STATUS_RANK = { draft: 0, proposed: 1, published: 2 } as const;

function linkKey(link: ExternalLink | undefined): string {
  return link ? `${link.program}\n${link.ticket}\n${link.url}` : "";
}

/**
 * An external link is one value, not text: the side that changed it wins,
 * ours when both did (specs/external-links/project-management-jira-linear-
 * etc.feature.md).
 */
function mergeLink(
  base: ExternalLink | undefined,
  ours: ExternalLink | undefined,
  theirs: ExternalLink | undefined,
): ExternalLink | undefined {
  const winner =
    linkKey(theirs) !== linkKey(base) && linkKey(ours) === linkKey(base) ? theirs : ours;
  return winner ? { ...winner } : undefined;
}

/** Merge one scenario present on both sides. */
function mergeScenario(base: Scenario | undefined, ours: Scenario, theirs: Scenario): Scenario {
  const baseTests = (base?.tests ?? []).join("\n");
  const tests =
    theirs.tests.join("\n") !== baseTests && ours.tests.join("\n") === baseTests
      ? theirs.tests
      : ours.tests;
  const link = mergeLink(base?.link, ours.link, theirs.link);
  return {
    name: ours.name,
    version: ours.version,
    // status is a one-way latch: the side further along the lifecycle wins,
    // so a concurrent publish (or propose) on either side sticks.
    status: STATUS_RANK[ours.status] >= STATUS_RANK[theirs.status] ? ours.status : theirs.status,
    body: mergeText(base?.body ?? "", ours.body, theirs.body),
    tests: [...tests],
    ...(link ? { link } : {}),
  };
}

/**
 * Merge two descendants of a common base. Text merges character-level (see
 * mergeText); structure merges per scenario. What still needs a human — one
 * side deleting a scenario the other edited — is a conflict, reported instead
 * of guessed at, and the existing resolution UI takes over as it does today.
 */
export function threeWayFeature(
  base: Feature,
  ours: Feature,
  theirs: Feature,
): ThreeWayResult {
  const name = mergeText(base.name, ours.name, theirs.name);
  const description = mergeText(base.description, ours.description, theirs.description);

  const b = byKey(base);
  const o = byKey(ours);
  const t = byKey(theirs);
  const scenarios: Scenario[] = [];
  for (const k of new Set([...b.keys(), ...o.keys(), ...t.keys()])) {
    const inBase = b.get(k);
    const inOurs = o.get(k);
    const inTheirs = t.get(k);
    if (inOurs && inTheirs) {
      scenarios.push(mergeScenario(inBase, inOurs, inTheirs));
    } else if (inOurs && !inTheirs) {
      if (!inBase) {
        scenarios.push(inOurs); // we added it
      } else if (sameScenario(inOurs, inBase)) {
        // they deleted it and we left it alone: the deletion stands
      } else {
        return { ok: false, conflict: `scenario ${k} was edited here and deleted there` };
      }
    } else if (inTheirs && !inOurs) {
      if (!inBase) {
        scenarios.push(inTheirs); // they added it
      } else if (sameScenario(inTheirs, inBase)) {
        // we deleted it and they left it alone: the deletion stands
      } else {
        return { ok: false, conflict: `scenario ${k} was deleted here and edited there` };
      }
    }
  }
  const link = mergeLink(base.link, ours.link, theirs.link);
  // The assignment merges like a link: one va

=== driftSync.ts
// The sync-tick side of the Automerge system-of-record (see reload.ts):
//
// - autoResolveMergeConflicts: a git pull that conflicts on `.feature.md`
//   files no longer goes straight to the resolution dialog. Peers on this
//   app version shipped their edits as chunk files too, which merged cleanly
//   in the same pull — so the merged document already holds both sides, and
//   the conflicted markdown is just the projection needing to be rewritten.
//   Plain-markdown edits (older app versions, GitHub PRs) have no ops to
//   merge, so they go through a scenario-level three-way merge against the
//   git merge base. Only both-sides-edited-the-same-scenario remains a human
//   decision, surfaced through the existing conflict UI.
//
// - importDrift: markdown that changed without its document — an external
//   GitHub edit, an old-version peer's push, or a feature that predates its
//   document — is absorbed (or first imported) into the doc, and the file is
//   rewritten to the doc's projection. Drift that would tamper with a
//   published scenario is refused and the file repaired from the doc.

import type * as A from "@automerge/automerge";

const EMPTY = { name: "", description: "", scenarios: [] };

export interface AutoResolveResult {
  /** Conflicted files this pass resolved. */
  resolved: string[];
  /** Conflicted files left for the resolution dialog, with the reason. */
  remaining: Map<string, string>;
  message: string;
}

/**
 * Resolve what a merged document (or a three-way merge) can resolve of a
 * conflicted pull. Resolving the last conflicted file completes the merge
 * commit and pushes (backend resolveConflict semantics).
 */
export async function autoResolveMergeConflicts(
  dataDir: string,
  conflicted: string[],
): Promise<AutoResolveResult> {
  return withSpan(
    "sync.auto_resolve_conflicts",
    { "specdriven.dir": dataDir, "sync.conflicted_files": conflicted.length },
    async (span) => {
      const conflictedSet = new Set(conflicted);
      const resolved: string[] = [];
      const remaining = new Map<string, string>();
      for (const path of conflicted) {
        const why = await autoResolveOne(dataDir, path, conflictedSet);
        if (why === null) {
          resolved.push(path);
          log.info(`auto-merged conflicted ${path}`);
        } else {
          remaining.set(path, why);
          log.warn(`conflict on ${path} left for manual resolution`, { "sync.reason": why });
        }
      }
      span.setAttributes({
        "sync.resolved_files": resolved.length,
        "sync.remaining_files": remaining.size,
      });
      const parts = [];
      if (resolved.length > 0) parts.push(`auto-merged ${resolved.length} conflicted file(s)`);
      for (const [path, why] of remaining) parts.push(`${path}: ${why}`);
      return { resolved, remaining, message: parts.join("; ") };
    },
  );
}

/**
 * True when the other side of the merge added chunk files for this document.
 * `docDir` is where that document's chunks live — a feature's or a page's,
 * which are different trees, so the caller passes the right one.
 */
async function theirsBroughtChunks(dataDir: string, docDir: string): Promise<boolean> {
  const changed = await diffNames(dataDir, "HEAD", "MERGE_HEAD", docDir);
  for (const path of changed) {
    if (!path.endsWith(".bin")) continue;
    // The diff lists differences in both directions; a chunk of theirs is one
    // that exists in MERGE_HEAD and not in our HEAD.
    if ((await existsIn(dataDir, "MERGE_HEAD", path)) && !(await existsIn(dataDir, "HEAD", path))) {
      return true;
    }
  }
  return false;
}

/**
 * Resolve one conflicted path; null on success, else why it was left.
 *
 * For a conflicted `.feature.md` the decision tree is:
 * - The other peer runs this app version (its pull delivered chunk files for
 *   this feature): both sides' markdown is derivative — the merged document
 *   already holds both sides' operations, and its projection is the
 *   resolution. This is the common case, and it merges concurrent edits to
 *   the same scenario at character level.
 * - The other peer sent only markdown (older app version, GitHub edit): its
 *   changes have no operations to merge, so they fold in through a
 *   scenario-level three-way merge against the git merge base.
 * - Concurrent first import (signature: this feature's docid file conflicted
 *   in the same merge): merging the two lineages' chunks picked one side's
 *   content wholesale, so both sides' markdown folds back in three-way.
 */
async function autoResolveOne(
  dataDir: string,
  path: string,
  conflictedSet: Set<string>,
): Promise<string | null> {
  const docidOf = featureFileOfDocidPath(path);
  if (docidOf !== null) {
    // Two peers imported the same feature concurrently and minted different
    // identities. The chunks of both lineages merged in this same pull, and
    // Automerge already picked the winning lineage deterministically — the
    // docid file just records that winner's identity.
    const fileName = docidOf;
    const loaded = await loadFeatureDoc(dataDir, fileName);
    if (loaded === null) return "document did not load";
    await resolveConflict(dataDir, path, `${loaded.doc.id}\n`);
    return null;
  }
  // A markdown page — a roadmap page, a task, a notebook entry
  // (specs/app/engineering-notebook.feature.md "Entries are shared through
  // git"): the same decision tree as a feature's below, minus the scenarios.
  // Its document holds both sides' operations when the other peer runs this
  // version; markdown that arrived without operations folds in three-way.
  if (isPageFolderPath(path) && isPageFile(path)) {
    return autoResolvePage(dataDir, path);
  }
  if (!path.endsWith(".feature.md") || path.startsWith(`${AUTOMERGE_ROOT}/`)) {
    return "not a feature file";
  }
  const loaded = await loadFeatureDoc(dataDir, path);
  if (loaded === null) return "no document behind it";
  const theirsText = (await stageVersion(dataDir, path, 3)) ?? (await theirsVersion(dataDir, path));
  if (theirsText === null) return "deleted on one side";
  const baseText = await stageVersion(dataDir, path, 1);
  const base = baseText === null ? EMPTY : parseFeature(baseText);
  const lineageRace = conflictedSet.has(`${docDirRel(path)}/docid`);
  const opsArrived = await theirsBroughtChunks(dataDir, docDirRel(path));

  let doc = loaded.doc;
  try {
    if (lineageRace) {
      const oursText = await stageVersion(dataDir, path, 2);
      if (oursText !== null && !isProjectionOf(oursText, doc)) {
        const own = threeWayFeature(base, docToFeature(doc), parseFeature(oursText));
        if (!own.ok) return own.conflict;
        doc = reconcile(doc, own.feature);
      }
    }
    if (!opsArrived || lineageRace) {
      const merged = threeWayFeature(base, docToFeature(doc), parseFeature(theirsText));
      if (!merged.ok) return merged.conflict;
      doc = reconcile(doc, merged.feature);
    }
  } catch

/**
 * Bring a document loaded from disk up to SCHEMA_VERSION: fold a v1 entry's
 * `published`/`proposed` booleans into `status` (v2), move the `scenarios`
 * list to `specs` and stamp each entry `type: "Scenario"` (v3). Applied on
 * load; the heads returned by loadFeatureDoc are the pre-migration heads, so
 * the migration ops ride whatever delta the caller saves next — a load that
 * never saves just migrates again next time.
 *
 * The v3 list move copies entries (ids kept) into a new list, so two peers
 * migrating the same document concurrently produce two `specs` lists and
 * Automerge picks the same winner on every peer — the foreign-lineage case
 * described at the top of this file, repaired by the markdown drift pass.
 */
function migrated(doc: A.Doc<FeatureDoc>): A.Doc<FeatureDoc> {
  const legacy = doc as unknown as A.Doc<LegacyFeatureDoc>;
  const entries = [...(legacy.specs ?? []), ...(legacy.scenarios ?? [])];
  const stale =
    doc.schemaVersion < SCHEMA_VERSION ||
    legacy.scenarios !== undefined ||
    legacy.specs === undefined ||
    entries.some((s) => s.status === undefined || s.type === undefined);
  if (!stale) return doc;
  return A.change(doc, (raw) => {
    const d = raw as unknown as LegacyFeatureDoc;
    if (d.schemaVersion < SCHEMA_VERSION) d.schemaVersion = SCHEMA_VERSION;
    if (d.specs === undefined) d.specs = [];
    if (d.scenarios !== undefined) {
      for (const entry of d.scenarios) {
        d.specs.push({
          id: entry.id,
          type: entry.type ?? "Scenario",
          name: entry.name,
          version: entry.version,
          status: legacyStatus(entry),
          body: entry.body,
          tests: [...entry.tests],
        });
      }
      delete d.scenarios;
    }
    for (const entry of d.specs) {
      if (entry.status === undefined) {
        entry.status = legacyStatus(entry);
        delete entry.published;
        delete entry.proposed;
      }
      if (entry.type === undefined) entry.type = "Scenario";
    }
  });
}

/**
 * The kinds of document the store holds: `features` for the `.feature.md`
 * documents, `docs` for the plain markdown pages of the special folders —
 * the roadmap's (specs/app/roadmap.feature.md), the tasks'
 * (specs/app/tasks.feature.md) and the notebook's — and a plugin's per-file
 * documents (the file-doc functions below, src/plugins/sdk/host.ts), and
 * `testing` for the test management module's one document
 * (specs/app/tests.feature.md), which has no markdown side at all — the
 * chunks are the only copy. The kind says which schema a document follows;
 * where it lives on disk follows from its file name alone (docDirRel and
 * friends below).
 */
....
function contentHash(bytes: Uint8Array): string {
  return createHash("sha256").update(bytes).digest("hex").slice(0, 16);
}

async function chunkFiles(dir: string): Promise<{ snapshots: string[]; changes: string[] }> {
  let entries: string[];
  try {
    entries = await readdir(dir);
  } catch {
    return { snapshots: [], changes: [] };
  }
  // Sorted for deterministic application order across peers.
  entries.sort();
  return {
    snapshots: entries.filter((f) => f.startsWith("snapshot-") && f.endsWith(".bin")),
    changes: entries.filter((f) => f.startsWith("changes-") && f.endsWith(".bin")),
  };
}

/**
 * Load a document by merging every chunk in its directory, or null when there
 * is none yet. `migrate` brings what disk holds up to the current schema (the
 * feature documents' only); the heads returned are the pre-migration ones, so
 * the migration ops ride whatever delta the caller saves next.
 */
async function loadDoc<T>(
  dataDir: string,
  kind: DocKind,
  fileName: string,
  migrate: (doc: A.Doc<T>) => A.Doc<T> = (doc) => doc,
): Promise<LoadedDoc<T> | null> {
  const dir = kindDir(dataDir, kind, fileName);
  const { snapshots, changes } = await chunkFiles(dir);
  if (snapshots.length === 0 && changes.length === 0) return null;

  let doc: A.Doc<T> | null = null;
  const skippedChunks: string[] = [];
  for (const file of [...snapshots, ...changes]) {
    let bytes: Uint8Array;
    try {
      bytes = await readFile(join(dir, file));
    } catch {
      skippedChunks.push(file);
      continue;
    }
    try {
      doc = doc === null ? A.load<T>(bytes) : A.loadIncremental(doc, bytes);
    } catch {
      skippedChunks.push(file);
    }
  }
  if (doc === null) return null;

  if (skippedChunks.length > 0) {
    // Skipping is deliberately non-fatal, but it must not be invisible: a
    // chunk that never loads anywhere means edits living only in that chunk
    // are not reaching this peer.
    log.warn(`skipped ${skippedChunks.length} unreadable chunk(s) for ${fileName}`, {
      "specdriven.file": fileName,
      "automerge.skipped_chunks": skippedChunks.join(","),
    });
  }
  const docId = (await readTextFile(join(dir, "docid")))?.trim() ?? (doc as { id?: string }).id ?? "";
  // Heads are captured before the schema migration on purpose — see migrated.
  const heads = A.getHeads(doc);
  return { doc: migrate(doc), heads, docId, skippedChunks };
}

/**
 * Load a feature's document, or null when the feature has none yet (a
 * pre-migration repo, or a plain-md write from an older app version).
 */
....
 * Persist changes made since `sinceHeads` (the heads a load returned) as one
 * incremental chunk, folding everything into a fresh snapshot once enough
 * change files pile up. A no-op delta writes nothing.
 */
async function saveDoc<T>(
  dataDir: string,
  kind: DocKind,
  fileName: string,
  doc: A.Doc<T>,
  sinceHeads: A.Heads,
): Promise<void> {
  const dir = kindDir(dataDir, kind, fileName);
  const delta = A.saveSince(doc, sinceHeads);
  if (delta.length === 0) return;
  await withSpan(
    "automerge.save_doc",
    { "specdriven.file": fileName, "automerge.delta_bytes": delta.length },
    async () => {
      await mkdir(dir, { recursive: true });
      await writeFile(join(dir, `changes-${contentHash(delta)}.bin`), delta);
      log.debug(`saved incremental chunk for ${fileName}`, {
        "automerge.delta_bytes": delta.length,
      });
      await compactDoc(dataDir, kind, fileName);
      if (kind === "features") emitDocSaved(dataDir, fileName);
    },
  );
}
....
}

/**
 * Fold snapshots and change files into a single fresh snapshot when the
 * directory has grown past COMPACT_AT change files. Old chunks are deleted
 * from the working tree; two peers compacting concurrently just leave two
 * snapshots for the next load to merge.
 */
async function compactDoc(dataDir: string, kind: DocKind, fileName: string): Promise<void> {
  const dir = kindDir(dataDir, kind, fileName);
  const { snapshots, changes } = await chunkFiles(dir);
  if (changes.length < COMPACT_AT) return;
  await withSpan(
    "automerge.compact_doc",
    { "specdriven.file": fileName, "automerge.change_chunks": changes.length },
    async () => {
      const loaded = await loadDoc<unknown>(dataDir, kind, fileName);
      if (loaded === null) return;
      const bytes = A.save(loaded.doc);
      const name = `snapshot-${contentHash(bytes)}.bin`;
      await writeFile(join(dir, name), bytes);
      const applied = new Set(loaded.skippedChunks);
      for (const file of [...snapshots, ...changes]) {
        // Only chunks that actually loaded are covered by the new snapshot.
        if (file !== name && !applied.has(file)) await rm(join(dir, file), { force: true });
      }
      log.debug(`compacted ${changes.length} change chunk(s) into a snapshot for ${fileName}`, {
        "automerge.snapshot_bytes": bytes.length,
      });
    },
  );
}

export async function compactFeatureDoc(dataDir: string, fileName: string): Promise<void> {
  return compactDoc(dataDir, "features", fileName);
}

export async function saveFeature(
  dataDir: string,
  fileName: string,
  content: string,
  scenario?: ScenarioTarget,
): Promise<SaveResult> {
  const name = safe(fileName);
  let proposed = markProposed(content);
  assertFeatureText(name, proposed);
  proposed = await foldDiskEdit(dataDir, name, proposed);
  await assertPublishedUnchanged(dataDir, name, proposed);
  const written = await writeFeatureFile(dataDir, name, proposed, scenario);
  // Hand the drafts (and the ids) back with the file content, or the client
  // would see the scenario it just saved vanish.
  return { status: "saved", ...(await featureView(dataDir, name, written)), message: "saved locally" };
}

/**
 * Fold an edit made to the `.feature.md` on disk — by hand, by an agent, by a
 * git checkout — that the reload cron has not absorbed into the document yet
 * (driftSync.ts importDrift) into a save's text. The save is otherwise
 * reconciled against the document alone and its projection written over the
 * file, so a disk edit landing between two sync ticks would be silently lost.
 *
 * The merge base is the document's projection: what the file held before the
 * disk edit. A disk edit that tampers with a published scenario is not folded
 * in — the drift pass refuses it and repairs the file from the document — and
 * one that changed the same text the editor did is refused with an error
 * rather than guessed at (threeWayFeatureStrict), so nothing is dropped
 * silently either way.
 */
async function foldDiskEdit(dataDir: string, fileName: string, text: string): Promise<string> {
  const onDisk = await readFeature(dataDir, fileName);
  if (onDisk === null || featureTextError(onDisk) !== null) return text;
  const loaded = await loadFeatureDoc(dataDir, fileName);
  if (loaded === null || isProjectionOf(onDisk, loaded.doc)) return text;
  const theirs = parseFeature(onDisk);
  try {
    assertPublishedKept(loaded.doc, theirs);
  } catch {
    return text;
  }
  const merged = threeWayFeatureStrict(
    parseFeature(projectFeature(loaded.doc)),
    parseFeature(text),
    theirs,
  );
  if (!merged.ok) {
    throw new Error(
      `${fileName} was edited on disk and here at the same time (${merged.conflict}) — ` +
        `the disk edit is kept; reopen the feature to pick it up`,
    );
  }
  log.info(`folded an unsynced disk edit of ${fileName} into a save`);
  return serializeFeature(merged.feature);
}

 * than a save of the feature without them — markdown cannot express a draft's
 * absence, and the next read would fold the draft straight back in. Local like

): Promise<SaveResult> {

  const loaded = await loadFeatureDoc(dataDir, name);
  const next = loaded === null ? null : removeDraftScenario(loaded.doc, target);
  if (loaded === null || next === null) {

export type ScenarioStatus = "published" | "proposed" | "draft";

/**
 * A link to the ticket that tracks a feature or a scenario in a project
 * management tool — Jira, Linear, etc.
 * (specs/external-links/project-management-jira-linear-etc.feature.md).
 * Written into the markdown as `[<program>:<ticket>](<url>)`, e.g.
 * `[Jira:ABC-123](https://acme.atlassian.net/browse/ABC-123)`.
 */
export interface ExternalLink {
  /**
   * Short name of the tool: "Jira", "Linear", "GitHub"… One of the
   * `EXTERNAL_LINK_TYPES` the editor offers by name, or — for "Other" — the
   * name the user gave it.
   */
  program: string;
  /** The ticket id as the tool shows it: "ABC-123", "ENG-42", "#17". */
  ticket: string;
  url: string;
}

/**
 * The tools the editor's "Tool" dropdown offers; a link to any other tool is
 * "Other", and keeps the tool's own name in `program`.
 */
export const EXTERNAL_LINK_TYPES = ["GitHub", "Jira", "Linear", "Other"] as const;
export type ExternalLinkType = (typeof EXTERNAL_LINK_TYPES)[number];

/** The dropdown entry a link's program is — "Other" for any tool not offered by name. */
export function externalLinkTypeOf(program: string): ExternalLinkType {
  const name = program.trim().toLowerCase();
  return EXTERNAL_LINK_TYPES.find((t) => t !== "Other" && t.toLowerCase() === name) ?? "Other";
}

/**
 * A pull request implementing a feature or a scenario
 * (specs/external-links/related-pull-requests.feature.md). Held in the
 * Automerge document only — never parsed from or written to markdown.
 */
export interface PullRequestLink {
  url: string;
  /** The pull request's title, read from GitHub or typed; empty when unknown. */
  title: string;
}

export interface Scenario {
  name: string;
  version: number;
  status: ScenarioStatus;
  body: string;
  /**
   * The automated tests covering this scenario (specs/external-links/
   * test-traceability.feature.md), each as the text inside its `[test: … ]`
   * token: the GitHub url alone, or `<test name> : <url>` when the test's
   * function name is known — see parseTestRef / formatTestRef. Kept as
   * strings so the Automerge document and the file agree on the entry.
   */
  tests: string[];
  /**
   * The document's id for the scenario
   * (specs/datastore/automerge-store.feature.md) — the identity that survives
   * a rename, carried only on what the editor reads from the server. Private
   * to the document: never parsed from or written to markdown.
   */
  id?: string;
  /** The ticket tracking this scenario, if it has one. */
  link?: ExternalLink;
  /**
   * The pull requests implementing this scenario. Document-only, like `id`:
   * carried on what the editor reads from the server, never serialized.
   */
  prs?: PullRequestLink[];
}

export interface Feature {
  name: string;
  /** Free markdown between the feature heading and the first scenario. */
  description: string;
  scenarios: Scenario[];
  /** The ticket tracking the feature as a whole, if it has one. */
  link?: ExternalLink;
  /** The pull requests implementing the feature as a whole; document-only. */
  prs?: PullRequestLink[];
  /**
   * Name of the project member the feature is assigned to, if any — one of
   * the members listed in the data repo's `project-settings.json`
   * (src/server/members.ts). Absent when nobody is assigned.
   */
  assigned?: string;
}

/**
 * Shared identity slug: feature file stems (`<slug>.feature.md`) and deep-link
 * url segments (specs/navigation.spec.md) both use it. Lives here rather than
 * features.ts so the frontend can import it without pulling in node:fs.
 */
=== 634-770
export function sortScenarios(scenarios: Scenario[]): Scenario[] {
  return [...scenarios].sort((a, b) => a.name.localeCompare(b.name) || a.version - b.version);
}

/**
 * Why a text is not a well-formed feature file, or null when it is
 * (specs/crud-feature.spec.md "Feature file format"): it opens with the
 * feature heading — `# ` followed by the feature name — and nothing above it.
 * Reading stays lenient (a file with no heading still lists, named after its
 * stem); writing does not, so a bug upstream cannot commit a broken file.
 */
export function featureTextError(text: string): string | null {
  const first = text.split("\n").find((line) => line.trim() !== "");
  if (first === undefined) return "the file is empty";
  if (FEATURE_RE.test(first)) return null;
  if (/^#\s*$/.test(first)) return "the feature heading has no name";
  return `it must start with the feature heading "# <name>", not ${JSON.stringify(clip(first))}`;
}

/** Enough of a line to recognize it in an error message. */
function clip(line: string): string {
  return line.length > 40 ? `${line.slice(0, 40)}…` : line;
}

export function parseFeature(text: string): Feature {
  let name = "";
  const preamble: string[] = [];
  const scenarios: Scenario[] = [];
  let current: Scenario | null = null;
  const bodyLines: string[] = [];
  let inFence = false;

  const flush = () => {
    if (current) {
      const split = splitTests(bodyLines.join("\n").trim());
      const { text, link } = splitExternalLink(split.body);
      current.body = text;
      current.tests = split.tests;
      if (link) current.link = link;
      scenarios.push(current);
      bodyLines.length = 0;
    }
  };

  for (const line of text.split("\n")) {
    if (FENCE_RE.test(line)) {
      inFence = !inFence;
    } else if (!inFence) {
      const featureMatch = line.match(FEATURE_RE);
      if (featureMatch && !name && !current) {
        name = featureMatch[1]!;
        continue;
      }
      const scenarioMatch = matchScenario(line);
      if (scenarioMatch) {
        flush();
        current = {
          name: scenarioMatch.name,
          version: scenarioMatch.version,
          status: scenarioMatch.status,
          body: "",
          tests: [],
        };
        continue;
      }
    }
    if (current) bodyLines.push(line);
    else preamble.push(line);
  }
  flush();

  const { text: description, link, assigned } = splitFeatureMeta(preamble.join("\n").trim());
  const feature: Feature = { name, description, scenarios: sortScenarios(scenarios) };
  if (link) feature.link = link;
  if (assigned) feature.assigned = assigned;
  return feature;
}

function header(name: string, version: number, status: ScenarioStatus): string {
  return `## ${name} @v${version} [${status}]`;
}

export interface SerializeOptions {
  /**
   * Whether the feature's and scenarios' external links are written
   * (default true). The `.feature.md` projection passes the "Publish
   * external links" setting here; everything else — the editor wire format,
   * the conflict dialog — always carries the links.
   */
  links?: boolean;
}

export function serializeFeature(feature: Feature, { links = true }: SerializeOptions = {}): string {
  const parts = [`# ${feature.name}`];
  if (links && feature.link) parts.push(formatExternalLink(feature.link));
  if (feature.assigned) parts.push(formatAssigned(feature.assigned));
  if (feature.description) parts.push(feature.description);
  for (const s of feature.scenarios) {
    const chunks = [header(s.name, s.version, s.status)];
    if (links && s.link) chunks.push(formatExternalLink(s.link));
    if (s.body) chunks.push(s.body);
    if (s.tests.length > 0) chunks.push(testsLines(s.tests));
    parts.push(chunks.join("\n\n"));
  }
  return parts.join("\n\n") + "\n";
}

/** Identity of one scenario inside a feature file: heading name + version. */
export interface ScenarioPick {
  name: string;
  version: number;
}

 * renames that entry rather than filing the new name as a second scenario
 * (specs/editor-autosave.feature.md "Autosave Never Adds the Same Scenario
 * Twice"), which matters most for a draft: the

The model's proofs are only as good as this agreement.\n\nimport { test, expect } from \"bun:test\";\nimport { mkdir, writeFile } from \"node:fs/promises\";\nimport { join } from \"node:path\";\nimport {\n  mergeScenario as _unused,\n  threeWayFeature,\n  threeWayFeatureStrict,\n} from \"../../src/server/automerge/merge\";\nimport { assertPublishedKept, reconcile } from \"../../src/server/automerge/reconcile\";\nimport { docToFeature, projectFeature } from \"../../src/server/automerge/project\";\nimport { newFeatureDoc } from \"../../src/server/automerge/store\";\nimport { parseFeature, type Feature, type Scenario, type ScenarioStatus } from \"../../src/server/parser\";\nimport type { SpecEntry } from \"../../src/server/automerge/schema\";\n\nvoid _unused;\n\nconst LEAN_DIR = join(import.meta.dir, \"../../verification/lean\");\nconst ELAN_BIN = join(process.env.HOME ?? \"\", \".elan/bin\");\n\n// mulberry32: deterministic, so a mismatch reproduces from the seed.\nfunction rng(seed: number) {\n  let a = seed >>> 0;\n  return () => {\n    a = (a + 0x6d2b79f5) >>> 0;\n    let t = a;\n    t = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), t | 1);\n    t ^= t + Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 7), t | 61);\n    return ((t ^ (t >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;\n  };\n}\nconst next = rng(20261003);\nconst pick = <T>(xs: readonly T[]): T => xs[Math.floor(next() * xs.length)]!;\nconst chance = (p: number) => next() < p;\n\n// Names restricted to lowercase letters, digits and spaces so localeCompare\n// and Lean's code-point order agree (verification/lean/SpecDriven/Sync/Projection.lean).\nconst NAMES = [\"alpha\", \"beta\", \"gamma\", \"delta\", \"alpha 2\"] as const;\nconst BODIES = [\"\", \"one\", \"two\", \"one two\", \"one\\ntwo\"] as const;\nconst TESTS = [[], [\"t1\"], [\"t1\", \"t2\"], [\"t2\"]] as const;\nconst LINKS = [undefined, { program: \"Jira\", ticket: \"A-1\", url: \"https://j/A-1\" }, { program: \"Linear\", ticket: \"L-2\", url: \"https://l/L-2\" }] as const;\nconst MEMBERS = [undefined, \"ann\", \"bob\"] as const;\nconst STATUSES: ScenarioStatus[] = [\"draft\", \"proposed\", \"published\"];\n\nfunction scenario(name: string, version: number, status: ScenarioStatus): Scenario {\n  const s: Scenario = { name, version, status, body: pick(BODIES), tests: [...pick(TESTS)] };\n  const link = pick(LINKS);\n  if (link) s.link = { ...link };\n  return s;\n}\n\n/** A feature with distinct (name, version) keys, drafts optional. */\nfunction feature(opts: { drafts?: boolean; n?: number } = {}): Feature {\n  const n = opts.n ?? 1 : 2;\n    if (!keys.has(`${name}@${version}`)) f.scenarios.push(scenario(name, version, pick([\"proposed\", \"published\"] as const)));\n  }\n  return f;\n}\n\n/** Both sides changed the same text to different values: mergeText's\n * nondeterministic branch, which the model leaves opaque. */\nfunction textClash(base: Feature, ours: Feature, theirs: Feature): boolean {\n  const clash = (b: string, o: string, t: string) => o !== b && t !== b && o !== t;\n  if (clash(base.name, ours.name, theirs.name)) return true;\n  if (clash(base.description, ours.description, theirs.description)) return true;\n  const byKey = (f: Feature) => new Map(f.scenarios.map((s) => [`${s.name}@${s.version}`, s]));\n  const b = byKey(base);\n  const t = byKey(theirs);\n  for (const s of ours.scenarios) {\n    const other = t.get(`${s.name}@${s.version}`);\n    if (other && clash(b.get(`${s.name}@${s.version}`)?.body ?? \"\", s.body, other.body)) return true;\n  }\n  return false;\n}\n\n// JSON shape the Lean decoder expects: every optional present, null for absent.\nconst link = (l?: { program: string; ticket: string; url: string }) => (l ? import { cpSync, mkdtempSync } from "node:fs";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { join } from "node:path";

export async function git(dir: string, ...args: string[]) {
  const res = await $`git -C ${dir} ${args}`.quiet().nothrow();
  if (res.exitCode !== 0 && args[0] !== "pull") {
    throw new Error(`git ${args.join(" ")} failed: ${res.stderr.toString()}`);
  }
  return res;
}

export interface TwoClones {
  a: string;
  b: string;
  originDir: string;
  /** The origin's location, as callers should hand it to clone(). */
  originUrl: string;
}

const DEFAULT_SEED = { "specs/seed.feature.md": "# Seed\n" };

/**
 * A bare origin plus two clones a and b, both seeded and tracking origin/main,
 * as a copy of a template built once per process.
 */
export async function twoClones(
  root: string,
  seedFiles: Record<string, string> = DEFAULT_SEED,
): Promise<TwoClones> {
  const template = await templateFor(seedFiles);
  cpSync(template, root, { recursive: true });
  const a = join(root, "a");
  const b = join(root, "b");
  await Promise.all([repointOrigin(a, template, root), repointOrigin(b, template, root)]);
  const originDir = join(root, "origin.git");
  return { a, b, originDir, originUrl: originDir };
}

/**
 * A bare origin with one seeded commit and no working clone left behind —
 * for tests that only need something cloneable to point at. Copied from the
 * same template as twoClones, so it costs a copy rather than a build.
 *
 * `at` places it somewhere other than `<root>/origin.git`, for tests whose
 * url mapping depends on the path (e.g. `test/origin.git` standing in for
 * `github.com/test/origin`). A bare repo has no remote of its own, so unlike
 * twoClones the copy needs no rewriting.
 */
export async function seededOrigin(
  root: string,
  at = "origin.git",

Each one\nnames the Automerge behaviour it stands for and how it could be discharged.\n\n## Documents as change sets\n\nAn Automerge document is the set of changes merged into it. Merging two\ndocuments is the union of their change sets, and the document's observable\nstate is a function of that set alone: not of the order the changes arrived\nin, nor of how many times each arrived. That is the join-semilattice fact the\nstore relies on (\"merging the same operations twice is a no-op\",\nnetsync.ts). Here a change set is a `List Change` read up to membership\n(`ChangeSet.equiv`), which gives commutativity, associativity and idempotence\nof `++` for free, and `view` is the opaque interpretation.\n\n## Dependency closure\n\nA change names the heads it was made on top of (`deps`). Automerge's\n`loadIncremental` holds a change whose dependencies are missing as pending\nuntil they arrive, so a change set is fully applied only when it is\ndependency-closed. `saveSince doc heads` produces changes whose dependencies\nare all within `doc` (axiom `saveSince_deps`).\n\n## Text\n\n`updateText` is not an assignment: it diffs the current text against the new\none and mints insert and delete operations for the difference, under this\npeer's actor id. Two peers running `updateText` with the same new text on two\nforks of the same document mint two independent sets of inserts, and merging\nthe forks keeps both (axiom `Text.concurrent_update_grows`). This is the\nsingle most important assumption for hypothesis H2. It could be discharged by\na property test against `@automerge/automerge`: fork, `updateText` on both,\n`merge`, compare.\n-/\n\nnamespace SpecDriven.Sync\n\n/-- One Automerge change: its hash and the hashes it depends on. The protocol\nmodel does not simulate git. It takes what a pull delivered for one feature\npath as data (`Pulled`), in the terms `autoResolveOne` reads it:\n\n- `conflicted`: the path is in `conflictedFiles` after the pull.\n- `opsArrived`: `theirsBroughtChunks` \u2014 the other side added chunk files for\n  this document in the same merge.\n- `lineageRace`: this feature's `docid` file conflicted in the same merge.\n- `base`, `ours`, `theirs`: `stageVersion` 1, 2 and 3 of the path, parsed\n  (`theirsVersion` stands in for stage 3); `none` is \"absent on that side\".\n\nAssumptions about git itself, stated here rather than proved:\n\n1. **Chunk directories merge as a file-set union.** Chunk files are\n   content-addressed and write-once (`store.ts`), so two peers never change\n   the same chunk path and a merge adds both sides' files. Standard git.\n3. **A deletion on one side with no change on the other deletes.** This is\n   why \"deleted on one side\" is left for the dialog and why a compaction's\n   deletions of chunk files propagate.\n4. **`resolveConflict` on the last conflicted path completes the merge\n   commit and pushes**, so a peer that auto-resolved everything leaves no\n   merge in progress for the drift pass to skip.\n-/\n\nnamespace SpecDriven.Sync\n\n/-- What one pull delivered for one feature path. -/\nstructure Pulled where\n  conflicted : Bool\n  opsArrived : Bool\n  lineageRace : Bool\n  base : Option Feature\n  ours : Option Feature\n  theirs : Option Feature\n  deriving Repr\n\nend SpecDriven.Sync\nEOF\ncat > SpecDriven/Sync/Protocol.lean <<'EOF'\nimport SpecDriven.Sync.Git\nimport SpecDriven.Sync.Merge\nimport SpecDriven.Sync.Reconcile\nimport SpecDriven.Sync.Projection\n\n/-!\n# Protocol\n\nThe sync tick (`src/server/reload.ts` `syncDataDir`) and its two document\npasses (`src/server/automerge/driftSync.ts` `autoResolveOne`, `importDrift`)\nfor one feature on one peer, plus the save-time fold (`src/server/features.ts`\n`foldDiskEdit`).\n\nThe peer holds, for the feature, the document it loads from its chunk\ndirectory (`doc`) and the markdown on disk (`file`). 1	import SpecDriven.Sync.Git
2	import SpecDriven.Sync.Merge
3	import SpecDriven.Sync.Reconcile
4	import SpecDriven.Sync.Projection
5	
6	/-!
7	# Protocol
8	
9	The sync tick (`src/server/reload.ts` `syncDataDir`) and its two document
10	passes (`src/server/automerge/driftSync.ts` `autoResolveOne`, `importDrift`)
11	for one feature on one peer, plus the save-time fold (`src/server/features.ts`
12	`foldDiskEdit`).
13	
14	The peer holds, for the feature, the document it loads from its chunk
15	directory (`doc`) and the markdown on disk (`file`). Markdown is represented
16	by its parse (Projection.lean); `links` is this peer's "Publish external
17	links" setting.
18	-/
19	
20	namespace SpecDriven.Sync
21	
22	/-- The markdown file for a feature, as `importDrift` finds it. -/
23	inductive FileState where
24	  /-- No file: deleted outside the app. -/
25	  | absent
26	  /-- `featureTextError` is not null: skipped, never reaches the document. -/
27	  | malformed
28	  | text (f : Feature)
29	  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
30	
31	structure PeerFeature where
32	  doc : Option FeatureDoc
33	  file : FileState
34	  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
35	
36	/-- `EMPTY` in driftSync.ts. -/
37	def EMPTY : Feature := { name := "", description := "", scenarios := [] }
38	
39	/-- `autoResolveOne` for a conflicted `.feature.md` that has a document:
40	the resolved document and the markdown written as the resolution, or why
41	the path was left to the dialog. -/
42	def autoResolveOne (seed : Nat) (fresh : Nat → String) (doc : FeatureDoc) (pl : Pulled) :
43	    Except String (FeatureDoc × Feature) := do
44	  let some theirs := pl.theirs | throw "deleted on one side"
45	  let base := pl.base.getD EMPTY
46	  let doc1 ←
47	    if pl.lineageRace then
48	      match pl.ours with
49	      | some ours =>
50	        if !isProjectionOf ours doc then do
51	          let own ← threeWayFeature seed base (docToFeature doc) ours
52	          let (d, _) ← reconcile fresh doc own
53	          pure d
54	        else pure doc
55	      | none => pure doc
56	    else pure doc
57	  let doc2 ←
58	    if !pl.opsArrived || pl.lineageRace then do
59	      let merged ← threeWayFeature seed base (docToFeature doc1) theirs
60	      let (d, _) ← reconcile fresh doc1 merged
61	      pure d
62	    else pure doc1
63	  return (doc2, projectFeature doc2 true)
64	
65	/-- The `docid` branch of `autoResolveOne`: the loaded document's id wins. -/
66	def resolveDocid (doc : FeatureDoc) : String := doc.id
67	
68	/-- `importDrift` for one feature: import, skip, absorb or repair; then the
69	second loop drops the document of a feature whose file is gone. -/
70	def importDriftOne (fresh : Nat → String) (docId : String) (links : Bool) (s : PeerFeature) :
71	    PeerFeature :=
72	  match s.file with
73	  | .absent => { doc := none, file := .absent }
74	  | .malformed => s
75	  | .text text =>
76	    match s.doc with
77	    | none =>
78	      let d := importFeatureDoc docId fresh (parseFeature text)
79	      { doc := some d, file := .text (projectFeature d links) }
80	    | some d =>
81	      if isProjectionOf text d then s
82	      else
83	        let (d', _refused) := absorbDrift fresh d text
84	        { doc := some d', file := .text (projectFeature d' links) }
85	
86	/-- What a tick knows about this feature: what the pull delivered (if the
87	path conflicted) and whether a merge is still in progress once the resolve
88	pass has run. -/
89	structure TickInput where
90	  pulled : Option Pulled
91	  merging : Bool
92	
93	/-- The resolve pass of a tick on this feature. -/
94	def resolvePass (seed : Nat) (fresh : Nat → String) (s : PeerFeature) (inp : TickInput) : PeerFeature :=
95	  match inp.pulled, s.doc with
96	  | some pl, some d =>
97	    if pl.conflicted then
98	      match autoResolveOne seed fresh d pl with
99	      | .ok (d', f) => { doc := some d', file := .text f }
100	      | .error _ => s
101	    else s
102	  | _, _ => s
103	
104	/-- `syncDataDir`, for one feature: pull (given), resolve what the document
105	can resolve, then — only when no merge is in progress — the drift pass. The
106	commit-and-push at the end moves bytes, not documents. -/
107	def tick (seed : Nat) (fresh : Nat → String) (docId : String) (links : Bool)
108	    (s : PeerFeature) (inp : TickInput) : PeerFeature :=
109	  let s1 := resolvePass seed fresh s inp
110	  if inp.merging then s1 else importDriftOne fresh docId links s1
111	
112	/-- `foldDiskEdit` in features.ts: a save's text, with an unabsorbed disk
113	edit folded in, left alone, or refused. -/
114	def foldDiskEdit (seed : Nat) (d : FeatureDoc) (onDisk : FileState) (text : Feature) :
115	    Except String Feature :=
116	  match onDisk with
117	  | .absent => .ok text
118	  | .malformed => .ok text
119	  | .text disk =>
120	    if isProjectionOf disk d then .ok text
121	    else if !publishedKept d disk then .ok text
122	    else
123	      match threeWayFeatureStrict seed (parseFeature (projectFeature d true)) (parseFeature text) disk with
124	      | .ok merged => .ok (serializeFeature true merged)
125	      | .error e => .error s!"edited on disk and here at the same time ({e})"
126	
127	/-- The projection invariant a tick restores for one feature. -/
128	def Consistent (links : Bool) (s : PeerFeature) : Prop :=
129	  match s.file, s.doc with
130	  | .text f, some d => isProjectionOf f d = true
131	  | .text _, none => False
132	  | .absent, d => d = none
133	  | .malformed, _ => True
134	
135	end SpecDriven.Sync

    62	/-- The match plan: entry index ↦ target scenario, computed exactly as the

    76	def claimPass (entries : List SpecEntry) (target : Feature)
    77	    (candidate : Scenario → SpecEntry → Bool) (p : Plan) : Plan :=
    78	  (enumerate entries).foldl (fun p (i, e) =>
    79	    if p.has i then p

    82	      | none => p) p

    85	def idClaim (entries : List SpecEntry) (target : Feature) (scenario : Option ScenarioTarget)

    96	      if blocked then p else { matchAt := [(i, s)], matched := [s] }
    97	    | _, _ => p
    98	  | _ => p

   109	/-- One entry after the update pass: the entry and the ops that moved it to

Exit code 1
Write the informal statement as a doc comment above the Lean theorem.

Store layer:

- **T1 Order-independent load.** `loadDoc` over any permutation of the same
  dependency-closed chunk set yields the same `Doc`. Anchor: `chunkFiles`
  sorts, `loadDoc` folds. Spec: "Storage layout".
- **T2 Union convergence.** Two peers whose chunk sets have the same union
  after a pull load equal documents. Follows from T1 and semilattice axioms.
  Spec: "two peers edit the same scenario concurrently".
- **T3 Compaction safety.** `loadDoc (compactDoc s) = loadDoc s`; chunks in
  `skippedChunks` are never deleted; a concurrent chunk added by another peer
  on top of any sub-closure of `s` still loads to the same merged document
  after `compactDoc` on this peer. Anchor: `compactDoc`, the `applied` set.
- **T4 Double delivery is a no-op.** Applying a change set already contained
  in the document leaves it unchanged. Anchor: `netsync.ts` header. Spec:
  "optional live sync over websocket".
- **T5 Migration idempotence.** `migrated (migrated d) = migrated d`, and
  `migrated` leaves entry ids, names, versions, bodies and tests unchanged.
  Anchor: `store.ts` `migrated`. Spec: "Documents from before the status
  field".

Pure merge layer:

- **T6 Status latch.** `mergeScenario` yields a status at least as far along
  as either input and equal to one of them. Anchor: `STATUS_RANK`. Spec:
  "draft scenarios live in the document only", last sentence.
- **T7 Three-way identities.** `threeWayFeature base ours base` returns
  `ours`, and `threeWayFeature base base theirs` returns `theirs`, up to the
  normalization `docToFeature` performs. A conflict is returned exactly when
  one side deleted a scenario the other side changed. Anchor:
  `threeWayFeature`.
- **T8 Strict refines lenient.** When `threeWayFeatureStrict` returns ok its
  result equals `threeWayFeature`'s. When it conflicts, some text field was
  changed on both sides to different values. Same for the page variants.
  Anchor: `threeWayFeatureStrict`, `threeWayPageStrict`.
- **T9 Pages never conflict.** `threeWayPage` is total and every metadata
  field of its result equals one of the three inputs. Anchor:
  `threeWayPage`.

Reconcile layer:

- **T10 Published immutability.** If `reconcile doc target` returns without
  error and `force` is false, then for every entry `e` in `doc` with status
  published there is an entry in the result with the same id, same name,
  same version, status published and the same body. Anchor:
  `assertPublishedKept`, `reconcile`. Spec: "published scenarios stay
  immutable".
- **T11 Drafts survive markdown.** Every draft entry of `doc` that the match
  plan does not claim is present in the result with the same id and body and
  status draft. A claimed draft keeps its id. Anchor: the removal loop skips
  `status === "draft"`. Spec: "draft scenarios live in the document only".
- **T12 Reconcile reaches the target.** For a well-formed `target` (distinct
  (name, version) keys, no drafts), `projectFeature (reconcile doc target)`
  equals `serializeFeature (sortScenarios target)`. Anchor: `reconcile` doc
  comment, first sentence.
- **T13 Projection fixpoint.** `reconcile doc (parseFeature (projectFeature
  doc))` produces no operations: the saved delta is empty. Anchor:
  `saveDoc` "a no-op delta writes nothing". This is what makes a tick on an
  undrifted repo quiet.
- **T14 Identity stability under rename.** When `scenario.id` names an
  unpublished entry and the target holds a scenario with `scenario`'s name
  and version that no other entry carries, the result keeps that entry's id
  under the new name. Anchor: the `scenario?.id` claim block.
- **T15 Match plan determinism.** The match plan is a function of `doc`,
  `target` and `scenario` only. State and prove that no two target scenarios
  claim the same entry and no entry is claimed twice.

Protocol layer, over the transition system:

- **T16 Tick preserves the projection invariant.** Define
  `Consistent(repo)`: every committed `.feature.md` with a document is
  `isProjectionOf` that document in one of its two link forms. Prove that
  from any reachable state, one tick on a peer with no concurrent writes
  during the tick restores `Consistent` for its working tree, and a second
  tick with no new external input writes nothing. Anchor: `syncDataDir`,
  `importDrift`, `isProjectionOf`. Spec: "markdown edited outside the app".
- **T17 No absorption during merge.** `importDrift` is never invoked while
  `conflictedFiles` is non-empty. Prove it as a guard theorem over the tick.
  Anchor: `mergeInProgress`.
- **T18 Deletion carries intent.** After a pull that removed a feature file,
  the next tick removes its document directory and never recreates the file
  from the stale document. Anchor: `importDrift`'s second loop. Spec: "A
  feature file deleted outside the app".
- **T19 Refused drift repairs.** When `absorbDrift` refuses, the document is
  unchanged and the file is rewritten to the document's projection. Anchor:
  `importDrift`'s `refused` branch. Spec: "published scenarios stay
  immutable".
- **T20 Concurrent edits converge without a dialog.** Two peers on this app
  version edit the same scenario body, each saves, each pushes, the second
  pull conflicts on the markdown. `autoResolveOne` takes the ops-arrived
  branch and both edits appear in the resolved file on both peers,
  byte-identical. Spec: "two peers edit the same scenario concurrently".
- **T21 Lineage race resolution.** Two peers import the same feature
  concurrently with different docids. After the merge, `autoResolveOne` on
  the `docid` path writes the loaded document's id, and on the feature path
  takes the lineage-race branch so that every scenario present on either
  side and not conflicting survives. Spec: "a repo that predates
  documents".
- **T22 Disk edits are never dropped silently.** For `saveFeature`, exactly
  one of: the disk edit is folded into the save; the save fails with the
  strict-conflict error and the disk edit stays on disk; or the disk edit
  tampers with a published scenario and the next tick repairs it. Anchor:
  `foldDiskEdit`.

## Hypotheses to try to refute

These are places where the implementation may be weaker than the spec. For
each, build the trace in the transition system. If the model confirms the
problem, write the trace as a `bun test` under `tests/automerge/` that fails
against the real implementation, and record it in the report. Do not fix the
TypeScript; report.

  different scenario in the same file (no chunks). The other peer's pull
  conflicts. `autoResolveOne` sees `opsArrived` true, skips the three-way
  merge, and re-projects from the merged document. Does the hand edit

## Report format

`REPORT.md` has four sections and nothing else:

1. **Proved.** Theorem id, one-line informal statement, the Lean name.
2. **Disproved.** Theorem or hypothesis id, the counterexample as a numbered
   trace of peer actions, the failing bun test's path, and the spec sentence
   it violates.
3. **Open.** What is in `Open.lean`, and what blocked each one.
4. **Axioms.** Every axiom, where it lives, and the Automerge or git
   behaviour it stands for, with a note on how it could be discharged
   (for example by a property test against the real Automerge library).

Report outcomes faithfully. A theorem is proved only when `lake build`
passes with no `sorry` on its path. If a phase is cut short, say which
theorems were never attempted.

/-!
# Theorems: reconcile layer (T10–T15)

Anchors: `src/server/automerge/reconcile.ts`. The match plan is a `foldl`
over the entries; every theorem here goes through an invariant of that fold.

namespace SpecDriven.Sync

/-! ## List helpers -/

theorem foldl_inv {α β : Type} (f : β → α → β) (P : β → Prop) (l : List α) (b : β)
    (hb : P b) (hf : ∀ b a, a ∈ l → P b → P (f b a)) : P (l.foldl f b) := by
  induction l generalizing b with
  | nil => exact hb
  | cons a l ih =>
    simp only [List.foldl_cons]
    exact ih (f b a) (hf b a (by simp) hb) (fun b' a' ha' => hf b' a' (by simp [ha']))

theorem enumerateFrom_getElem? {α : Type} (l : List α) (n i : Nat) :
    (enumerateFrom n l)[i]? = l[i]?.map (fun a => (n + i, a)) := by
  induction l generalizing n i with
  | nil => simp [enumerateFrom]
  | cons a l ih =>
    cases i with
    | zero => simp [enumerateFrom]
    | succ i =>
      simp only [enumerateFrom, List.getElem?_cons_succ, ih]
      congr 1; funext x; simp; omega

theorem enumerate_getElem? {α : Type} (l : List α) (i : Nat) :
    (enumerate l)[i]? = l[i]?.map (fun a => (i, a)) := by
  simp [enumerate, enumerateFrom_getElem?]

theorem mem_enumerateFrom {α : Type} (l : List α) (n i : Nat) (a : α) :
    (i, a) ∈ enumerateFrom n l ↔ n ≤ i ∧ l[i - n]? = some a := by
  induction l generalizing n i with
  | nil => simp [enumerateFrom]
  | cons x l ih =>
    simp only [enumerateFrom, List.mem_cons, Prod.mk.injEq, ih]
    constructor
    · rintro (⟨rfl, rfl⟩ | ⟨h1, h2⟩)
      · simp
      · refine ⟨by omega, ?_⟩
        have : i - n = (i - (n + 1)) + 1 := by omega
        rw [this]; simpa using h2
    · rintro ⟨h1, h2⟩
      by_cases hn : i = n
      · subst hn; simp at h2; left; exact ⟨rfl, h2.symm⟩
      · right
        refine ⟨by omega, ?_⟩
        have : i - n = (i - (n + 1)) + 1 := by omega
        rw [this] at h2; simpa using h2

theorem mem_enumerate {α : Type} (l : List α) (i : Nat) (a : α) :
    (i, a) ∈ enumerate l ↔ l[i]? = some a := by
  simp [enumerate, mem_enumerateFrom]

theorem enumerate_length {α : Type} (l : List α) : (enumerate l).length = l.length := by
  simp only [enumerate]
  suffices h : ∀ n, (enumerateFrom n l).length = l.length from h 0
  induction l with
  | nil => intro n; rfl
  | cons a l ih => intro n; simp [enumerateFrom, ih]

/-! ## The match plan -/

/-- One `forEach` step of `claim`. -/
def claimStep (target : Feature) (cand : Scenario → SpecEntry → Bool) (p : Plan) (ie : Nat × SpecEntry) :
    Plan :=
  if p.has ie.1 then p
  else match target.scenarios.find? (fun s => !p.isMatched s && cand s ie.2) with
    | some s => { matchAt := p.matchAt ++ [(ie.1, s)], matched := p.matched ++ [s] }
    | none => p

theorem claimPass_eq (entries : List SpecEntry) (target : Feature) (cand : Scenario → SpecEntry → Bool)
    (p : Plan) : claimPass entries target cand p = (enumerate entries).foldl (claimStep target cand) p := rfl

theorem Plan.has_iff (p : Plan) (i : Nat) : p.has i = true ↔ ∃ s, (i, s) ∈ p.matchAt := by
  simp only [Plan.has, List.any_eq_true, beq_iff_eq]
  constructor
  · rintro ⟨⟨j, s⟩, h, rfl⟩; exact ⟨s, h⟩
  · rintro ⟨s, h⟩; exact ⟨(i, s), h, rfl⟩

theorem Plan.isMatched_iff (p : Plan) (s : Scenario) : p.isMatched s = true ↔ s ∈ p.matched := by
  simp [Plan.isMatched]

/-- The plan invariant: `matched` is the range of `matchAt`, every match
names a target scenario and an entry index, no index is claimed twice, no
scenario is claimed twice. -/
structure Plan.Inv (entries : List SpecEntry) (target : Feature) (p : Plan) : Prop where
  range : ∀ s, s ∈ p.matched ↔ ∃ i, (i, s) ∈ p.matchAt
  sound : ∀ i s, (i, s) ∈ p.matchAt → s ∈ target.scenarios ∧ i < entries.length
  idxNodup : (p.matchAt.map (·.1)).Nodup
  matchedNodup : p.matched.Nodup

theorem Plan.Inv.empty (entries : List SpecEntry) (target : Feature) : Plan.Inv entries target {} :=
  ⟨by simp, by simp, by simp, by simp⟩

theorem claimStep_inv (entries : List SpecEntry) (target : Feature) (cand : Scenario → SpecEntry → Bool)
    (p : Plan) (ie : Nat × SpecEntry) (hie : ie ∈ enumerate entries) (h : Plan.Inv entries target p) :
    Plan.Inv entries target (claimStep target cand p ie) := by
  obtain ⟨i, e⟩ := ie
  have hlen : i < entries.length := by
    have := (mem_enumerate entries i e).mp hie
    exact List.getElem?_eq_some_iff.mp this |>.1
  unfold claimStep
  split
  · exact h
  · rename_i hhas
    split
    · rename_i s hs
      have hpred := List.find?_some hs
      have hsmem := List.mem_of_find?_eq_some hs
      simp only [Bool.and_eq_true, Bool.not_eq_eq_eq_not, Bool.not_true] at hpred
      have hnot : s ∉ p.matched := fun hm => by
        have := (Plan.isMatched_iff p s).mpr hm; simp_all
      have hnoti : ∀ s', (i, s') ∉ p.matchAt := fun s' hm => by
        refine ⟨h.matchedNodup, (by simp), ?_⟩
        intro a ha b hb
        simp only [List.mem_singleton] at hb
        subst hb
        intro hab; subst hab; exact hnot ha
    · exact h

theorem claimPass_inv (entries : List SpecEntry) (target : Feature) (cand : Scenario → SpecEntry → Bool)
    (p : Plan) (h : Plan.Inv entries target p) : Plan.Inv entries target (claimPass entries target cand p) := by
  rw [claimPass_eq]
  exact foldl_inv _ (Plan.Inv entries target) _ p h
    (fun p ie hie hp => claimStep_inv entries target cand p ie hie hp)

theorem idClaim_inv (entries : List SpecEntry) (target : Feature) (scenario : Option ScenarioTarget) :
    Plan.Inv entries target (idClaim entries target scenario {}) := by
  unfold idClaim
  split
  · rename_i pick id
    dsimp only
    split
    · rename_i i e s hat hcl
      split
      · exact Plan.Inv.empty entries target
      · have hmem := List.mem_of_find?_eq_some hat
        have hlen : i < entries.length :=
          (List.getElem?_eq_some_iff.mp ((mem_enumerate entries i e).mp hmem)).1
        have hs := List.mem_of_find?_eq_some hcl
        exact ⟨by simp, by simp_all, by simp, by simp⟩
    · exact Plan.Inv.empty entries target
  · exact Plan.Inv.empty entries target

/-- **T15 Match plan determinism and injectivity.** The plan is a function of
the document, the target and the editor's hint (it is a `def`), it names each
entry at most once and each target scenario at most once, and it only ever
names scenarios of the target. Anchor: `claim`, `matchAt`, `matched`. -/
theorem T15_plan_inv (entries : List SpecEntry) (target : Feature) (scenario : Option ScenarioTarget) :
    Plan.Inv entries target (plan entries target scenario) := by
  unfold plan
  exact claimPass_inv _ _ _ _ (claimPass_inv _ _ _ _ (idClaim_inv entries target scenario))

/-! ## What `applyPlan` does with the plan -/

theorem Plan.at?_none_of_not_has (p : Plan) (i : Nat) (h : p.has i = false) : p.at? i = none := by
  simp only [Plan.at?, Option.map_eq_none_iff, List.find?_eq_none]
  intro ⟨j, s⟩ hj hji
  have : p.has i = true := (Plan.has_iff p i).mpr ⟨s, by simp_all⟩
  simp_all

theorem Plan.at?_some_mem (p : Plan) (i : Nat) (s : Scenario) (h : p.at? i = some s) : (i, s) ∈ p.matchAt := by
  simp only [Plan.at?, Option.map_eq_some_iff] at h
  obtain ⟨⟨j, s'⟩, hj, hs⟩ := h
  have hmem := List.mem_of_find?_eq_some hj
  have hp := List.find?_some hj
  simp only [beq_iff_eq] at hp hs
  subst hp; subst hs; exact hmem

theorem updateEntry_none (p : Plan) (i : Nat) (e : SpecEntry) (h : p.at? i = none) :
    updateEntry p i e = (e, []) := by
  simp [updateEntry, h]

theorem updateEntry_some (p : Plan) (i : Nat) (e : SpecEntry) (m : Scenario) (h : p.at? i = some m) :
    (updateEntry p i e).1 = { e with name := m.name, version := m.version, status := m.status,
                                      body := m.body, tests := m.tests, link := m.link } := by
  simp [updateEntry, h]

theorem updatedSpecs_getElem? (p : Plan) (specs : List SpecEntry) (i : Nat) :
    (updatedSpecs p specs)[i]? = specs[i]?.map (fun e => updateEntry p i e) := by
  simp only [updatedSpecs, List.getElem?_map, enumerate_getElem?, Option.map_map]
  rfl
